Summary of the implementation in `cahn-hilliard/cahn_hilliard.cpp`:\n\n**Decomposition** — 3D Cartesian block distribution (`MPI_Cart_create`, with a custom factorization that minimizes halo surface while keeping the contiguous x-dimension as long as possible for vectorization). Each rank stores its block with a one-cell halo.\n\n**Boundary conditions** — the serial code's clamped (zero-gradient) stencil is reproduced by mirroring the adjacent interior plane into halo cells at the physical domain border, so a single uniform 7-point stencil is used everywhere; the branchy per-point `computeLaplacian` is gone and inner loops are contiguous and vectorizable.\n\n**Communication** — two halo exchanges per step (`c` before the chemical potential, `mu` before the update), using committed `MPI_Type_create_subarray` face types and non-blocking `Isend`/`Irecv`. The interior of the block is computed while the halos are in flight; only the outer shell waits on `MPI_Waitall`.\n\n**Output semantics** — validation min/max/NaN checks are `Allreduce`d, `-r` output is assembled on rank 0 in global linear order and fed to the unchanged `print_results`, so sum/hash/samples are bit-identical to the serial version. Only rank 0 prints; timing is the max over ranks.\n\nVerification against a serial build of the original code:\n- `64³/20 it`, `48×37×29/13 it`, `4×3×2/5 it` — identical `Sum`, `Min`, `Max`, samples and FNV `Hash` at 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 64 ranks.\n- Rank counts that can't tile the grid (e.g. 17 ranks on 4×3×2) fall back to the largest usable subset rather than aborting, still producing identical results.\n\nScaling on this 128-core node, 256³ / 20 steps (serial original: 166 MCU/s):\n\n| ranks | 1 | 2 | 4 | 8 | 32 | 64 | 128 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| MCU/s | 290 | 486 | 1020 | 1303 | 3072 | 6320 | 12065 |\n\nSingle-rank is already 1.75× the original from the kernel rewrite; 128 ranks is ~73× the serial baseline. Beyond 128 (hyperthreads) it saturates, as expected for a bandwidth-bound stencil.\n\n`CMakeLists.txt` now requires MPI (`find_package(MPI REQUIRED COMPONENTS CXX)`, linking `MPI::MPI_CXX`); executable name and flags are unchanged, and no new files or external dependencies were added."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":236,"cache_read_input_tokens":61561,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":236},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0f3853bd-509c-41a5-b6b0-80814a3b9913","uuid":"16c9f64e-a935-48bb-b082-2e087ec0e605","timestamp":"2026-09-13T23:22:19.151Z","request_id":"req_011Cf2JErosrX71PBJhcQ8ZG"}

Each rank stores its block with a one-cell halo.\n\n**Boundary conditions** — the serial code's clamped (zero-gradient) stencil is reproduced by mirroring the adjacent interior plane into halo cells at the physical domain border, so a single uniform 7-point stencil is used everywhere; the branchy per-point `computeLaplacian` is gone and inner loops are contiguous and vectorizable.\n\n**Communication** — two halo exchanges per step (`c` before the chemical potential, `mu` before the update), using committed `MPI_Type_create_subarray` face types and non-blocking `Isend`/`Irecv`. The interior of the block is computed while the halos are in flight; only the outer shell waits on `MPI_Waitall`.\n\n**Output semantics** — validation min/max/NaN checks are `Allreduce`d, `-r` output is assembled on rank 0 in global linear order and fed to the unchanged `print_results`, so sum/hash/samples are bit-identical to the serial version. Only rank 0 prints; timing is the max over ranks.\n\nVerification against a serial build of the original code:\n- `64³/20 it`, `48×37×29/13 it`, `4×3×2/5 it` — identical `Sum`, `Min`, `Max`, samples and FNV `Hash` at 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 64 ranks.\n- Rank counts that can't tile the grid (e.g. 17 ranks on 4×3×2) fall back to the largest usable subset rather than aborting, still producing identical results.\n\nScaling on this 128-core node, 256³ / 20 steps (serial original: 166 MCU/s):\n\n| ranks | 1 | 2 | 4 | 8 | 32 | 64 | 128 |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| MCU/s | 290 | 486 | 1020 | 1303 | 3072 | 6320 | 12065 |\n\nSingle-rank is already 1.75× the original from the kernel rewrite; 128 ranks is ~73× the serial baseline.
